# 10db Accounts in the Daemon and API Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Every project runs on a known billing account. Each session proves it is that account before it sends a prompt. Projects on different accounts run in parallel under each account's own lane cap and usage limits. A metered account stops starting sessions at its monthly dollar cap. A project can move to another account with work in flight, by hand, from its usage-limit card, or on its own to a fallback account the builder named; it is offered the way back once the limit resets. Tokens and dollars are visible per account and per project. Agents can't casually read another account's credentials.

**Architecture:**

- **Data.**
  - A new `accounts` table: kind, label, payer, expected identity, config directory, Keychain secret reference, secret fingerprint, settings, lane cap, monthly cap and `archived`.
  - `projects.account_id` replaces `billing_mode`.
  - `sessions` gains `account_id` and `cached_tokens`, and `cost_usd` becomes null for subscriptions.
  - Usage is a query over sessions. The daemon seeds one subscription account, the default account labelled "Claude plan", on the builder's current Claude Code login (the login shell's `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, else Claude Code's default directory) and makes it the default for new projects. Every other account, keyed kinds included, gets its own empty config directory under the data directory.
- **Launch.** `environment/` gains the Keychain module and the **launcher**, the one module that knows the account variable names.
  - It turns an account into a session's launch: the env, the model to request (through the model map) and what the session must report. It reads a keyed account's secret from the Keychain at each start and compares its fingerprint.
  - It also builds the **project env**: the login env with every billing switch stripped. The gate and the repo's MCP servers get that, and never the account's credentials.
- **Verification.** A pure `sessions/account-check.ts` decides whether what Claude Code reports matches the account: `accountInfo()` before the prompt (identity, plus key source and provider), then the key source and model at the first `init`. The real and the fake adapter both use it. Mid-session credential errors are refusals too, and so is a subscription whose `accountInfo()` answers nothing (it reads as signed out). A refusal pauses the account, and the workstream waits in `PAUSED_LIMIT`; the workstream doesn't fail. A model mismatch fails the workstream, not the account.
- **Accounts runtime.** A new `accounts/` daemon module owns:
  - account pauses (replacing provider pauses), keyed by account, with three reasons: usage limit, monthly cap and refused;
  - the monthly cap, checked against the calendar month in local time on the injected clock;
  - the cards and the preparer. The preparer probes an unready account's identity at most once a minute (and not while its Confirm card is open), records the default account's current login with a card that names it, and asks the builder to confirm any other;
  - staggered subscription starts;
  - the account service behind the HTTP routes.
- **Scheduling.** The scheduler launches a workstream only when its account is ready, not paused, under its monthly cap and under its lane cap. Within a lane, the runner checks readiness, the pause and the cap again before every further session; a lane whose account became unready goes back to `QUEUED` and asks the preparer.
- **Moving accounts mid-work.** `PATCH /projects/:id` moves a project with work in flight. A running session finishes on the account it started on; each lane's next session runs on the project's account at that moment.
  - A conversation lives in its account's config directory, so a lane whose next action would resume one from another account starts a fresh session instead. It is briefed from the step's latest handoff, as after a failed resume, and the builder's answer or a gate failure the lane was fixing still reaches it.
  - The usage-limit card offers "Continue on <account>". A project may name a fallback account: when a session reports its account's usage at the project's threshold (95% by default), the project's running lanes get the handoff nudge and it moves there, or, with no usable fallback, the builder is asked. A hard limit moves it at once.
  - A project that left an account for its usage limit remembers it (`projects.moved_from_account_id`, `move_back_at`), and 10db asks once that limit resets whether to move it back.
- **Guard.** In every step kind, agents can't run Keychain commands, can't dump the environment, can't reference a credential variable, and can't touch any path under 10db's data directory or the default account's own sign-in (`~/.claude`, `~/.claude.json` and the login shell's `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`).
- **API and UI.** These routes come from accounts spec §7: `GET`/`POST /accounts`, `PATCH /accounts/:id`, `POST /accounts/:id/archive`, `PUT /accounts/:id/secret`, and `GET /accounts/:id/signin`, `/identity` and `/usage`. `PATCH /projects/:id` takes `accountId`, `fallbackAccountId` and `usageHandoffAt`. The desktop's new-project form and the Billing section get an account picker, and Billing a fallback picker; every picker shows an account's identity beside its label ("Claude plan · you@example.com"). Removing `billingMode` forces the account pickers. The Accounts screen itself is the next plan.

**Tech Stack:** As in M2a. No new dependencies: the Keychain is the macOS `security` tool through `node:child_process`, and usage is SQL over `sessions`.

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-17-10db-accounts-design.md` (merged). It extends `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-13-10db-factory-loop-design.md` §4, §5.2 point 7, §6 and §8. Spike evidence: `docs/architecture/research/spike-12-accounts.md` (on `main`). This plan implements:

- §3 every decision, as amended by "Spec amendments" below;
- §4 data model (Task 2; the project's move and fallback columns in Tasks 10 and 11);
- §5 launching and checking a session (Tasks 3, 5 and 6);
- §6 scheduling, caps and usage (Tasks 2 and 4), and moving a project between accounts (Tasks 9, 10 and 11);
- §7 API (Task 8, with the project's fallback in Task 11), plus the project's account and fallback pickers (Task 12);
- §8 testing (every task; the manual live check is Task 14);
- §10 point 4, the system-check warning (Task 3).

**Not in this plan:**

- **The Accounts Settings screen** (spec §7 "Settings (M2b)", §12 point 4): it lists accounts with kind, verified identity and this month's usage against the cap, and shows the sign-in steps. It is **the next plan**, `10db-10-accounts-settings`. It fills the Accounts slot M2b Part A left at `/settings/accounts` and needs only the routes Task 8 builds. Until it ships:
  - accounts other than the default one are created through the API (Task 13 documents how);
  - identities are confirmed from the inbox card Task 6 raises.
- Per-client invoicing, routing one project across several accounts at once, Codex accounts and OS-level isolation (spec §1 "Out of scope").
- **Carrying the conversation across accounts** (spec §11): copying a cut-off conversation into the new account's config directory and resuming it there instead of starting fresh. A spike checks whether the SDK's `forkSession` and `getSessionMessages` honour a per-account config directory, whether Bedrock resumes a subscription's transcript, and what it costs; a small follow-up plan uses it if it passes. Task 13 records it in the open items.

**Plan series:** `00-spikes` … `06-m2a-teams-and-routing`, `07-m2b-board-and-settings`, `08-batch-delivery`, **`09-accounts` (this file)**, then `10-accounts-settings` (not yet written).

**Executes after** both of these are on `main`:

1. The batch delivery plan (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-21-10db-08-batch-delivery.md`, on `origin/docs/batch-delivery-design`). This plan builds on:
   - its `batches` table and `projects.delivery`;
   - the scheduler's asynchronous `tick()`, `idle()` and batch launch rule;
   - `GuardInput.delivery` and the `gh` allowlist;
   - the `delivery/` module, `buildNetworkEnv` and the shipper's `sessionEnv`;
   - the inbox's `onFlagAnswer` hook.
2. M2b Part A (`feat/m2b-board-and-settings`). This plan builds on:
   - `board_columns`;
   - the Settings shell at `/settings/$section`, with its Accounts slot and its Billing section's account-picker slot;
   - `views/board/new-project-form.tsx`;
   - the project General form.

The spike 12 document (`docs/architecture/research/spike-12-accounts.md`, with its open-questions row and the `architecture-docs.test.ts` entry) is on `main`; Tasks 13 and 14 cite and update it. Its refresh rerun (the expiry-recording probe) lands separately; if it hasn't when Task 14 runs, leave the refresh row as it is.

This plan was written against `origin/main` at `2b3d811` plus those two branches as of 2026-10-22. Where a file, signature or anchor it names doesn't match the code, trust the code, adapt the step and say so in the task's report.

**Migration baseline.** Until the first published release, a schema change regenerates the baseline instead of adding a migration (`packages/db/AGENTS.md`, decision 67). Tasks 2, 10, 11 and 12 regenerate it. Delete any local `.dev/data` after each, because the daemon refuses a database made under an older baseline and exits with code 3.

## Decisions made in the plan

The spec leaves these open; the plan decides them. Task 13 records the lasting ones in `docs/architecture/decisions.md`.

1. **The default account lives where the builder's login is.** The seeded default account's `configDir` is the login shell's `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` when it sets an absolute path, and otherwise null, meaning Claude Code's default directory (`~/.claude`). Either way it is the builder's current login. The launcher sets `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` for the default account like any other directory, and leaves it unset only for null. A relative `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` names no one directory, so it is ignored, and the daemon logs a warning naming the variable (never its value) when it seeds the default account on Claude Code's default directory instead.
   - The first time work needs it, the preparer probes that directory and records the email it finds as `expectedIdentity`, because that login is "the builder's current login" (spec §3). It also raises a card that holds nothing: "Claude plan runs as <email>", with the email in its detail and the sign-in command for switching logins.
   - Every account created through the API, keyed kinds included, gets its own empty directory under `<dataDir>/accounts/<id>/claude` (mode 700). A keyed session then never reads the default account's `~/.claude.json`: no saved login to fall back to, and no key approvals or config shared with the default account. A subscription's identity is recorded only when the builder confirms it (a card's Confirm, or `PATCH /accounts/:id`).
2. **Keyed identity is a key fingerprint** (spike 12: a gateway reports no organization). `accounts.secret_fingerprint` is `sha256:` plus the first 16 hex digits of the secret's SHA-256.
   - `PUT /accounts/:id/secret` records it.
   - Every session start reads the Keychain secret and compares its fingerprint. A missing secret, or one changed outside 10db, refuses the session.
   - `expectedIdentity` is an optional organization for API-key and Bedrock accounts, compared only when set. A gateway takes none. `secretSet` means a fingerprint is recorded.
3. **Account refusals pause the account, not the workstream.** These problems are the account's, not the work's: identity mismatch, wrong credentials (key source or provider), signed out, secret missing or changed, cannot bill (a billing error or an account on hold) and unconfirmed.
   - The workstream goes to `PAUSED_LIMIT` and keeps its next action. It resumes the conversation when the session had started.
   - The account is paused with reason `refused` until the builder acts: `PATCH`, a new secret, or a card's Confirm or Resume. A Resume that fixed nothing meets the same refusal at the next session, and the card comes back.
   - On a subscription, an `accountInfo()` that rejects or never answers is `signed_out` too: a signed-out directory may report nothing at all, and its fix is the sign-in command.
   - A model mismatch, a keyed account's missing `accountInfo()`, and the repo's settings problem still fail the workstream as before (decision 47). A model mismatch is the work's problem, not the account's: the credentials were right, so pausing the account would hold its other lanes for nothing.
4. **Unready accounts never launch, and are prepared, never refused.** A subscription with no confirmed identity, or a keyed account with no secret, is unready.
   - The scheduler leaves its work `QUEUED` and calls the preparer, which raises one card. For a subscription with no identity it first probes, at most once per `PREPARE_RETRY_MS` (60 s) per account, and not at all while that project's Confirm card for the account is open.
   - A lane that finds its account unready before a further session goes back to `QUEUED` with its next action kept, through `PAUSED_LIMIT` because the state machine allows no shorter path, and calls the preparer. The account is not paused.
5. **Model check.** At the first `init`:
   - A requested alias (`MODEL_ALIAS_PATTERN`, `^[a-z]+$`, the one pattern the contracts' model map and aliases also use) matches a reported model whose ID contains `claude-<alias>`. That covers `claude-haiku-5-5`, a Bedrock `us.anthropic.claude-haiku-…` and a 1M-context suffix.
   - An ID from the model map must equal what is reported.
6. **Usage accounting.**
   - A session's usage belongs to the calendar month (local time) in which the session started.
   - Cached tokens are cache reads. Input is uncached input plus cache creation.
   - `costUsd` is the SDK's cumulative `total_cost_usd` for metered kinds, and null for subscriptions.
7. **Monthly cap.** The scheduler checks it before each launch, and the runner before each further session in a lane (handoff, next step, fix).
   - At or over the cap, the account is paused (`monthly_cap`) until the first instant of the next local month. One card is raised, and its body is rewritten with the spend as each running session ends.
   - Raising the cap, or any save, resumes the account. The next check pauses it again if it is still over.
8. **Pauses.** One pause per account, persisted in `app_settings` as `account_pause.<id>`.
   - A pause with no end (`refused`) outranks one that ends; otherwise the later end wins.
   - Only a usage-limit pause stops the account's running lanes. A cap or refused pause lets running sessions finish.
9. **Staggered subscription starts.** On a subscription account, one session starts at a time: from just before `adapter.start` until the session reports `started` or `ended`. Identity probes take the same slot.
   - This is the spec's fallback while the refresh question is open (spike 12 refresh check: partial).
   - The lane cap default stays 4.
10. **The project env.** The gate, the shipping gate and the expansion of the repo's MCP servers get the project env: the login env with every billing switch and 10db variable stripped, and no account variables. Only a session gets its account's credentials. This amends decision 44.
11. **Stripped from every session.** In addition to today's list: `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` and `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN`. The launcher then sets `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` from the account's own `configDir`; the login shell's value reaches only the default account's seed. A Bedrock session also drops the login shell's `AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID`, `AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY`, `AWS_SESSION_TOKEN`, `AWS_PROFILE`, `AWS_DEFAULT_PROFILE`, `AWS_REGION` and `AWS_DEFAULT_REGION`. Nothing billing-related comes from the login shell any more, including `AWS_BEARER_TOKEN_BEDROCK` and `ANTHROPIC_BEDROCK_BASE_URL`.
12. **Secret handling.**
    - A secret is 8 to 8192 characters of printable ASCII with no space, quote or backslash.
    - It reaches the Keychain through `security -i` on standard input, never on a command line. It is read back and compared before its fingerprint is recorded.
    - Keychain failures answer 409 with a message that names the item, never the value.
13. **Guard additions** beyond the spec's list:
    - the default account's own sign-in, which lives outside the data directory: `~/.claude`, `~/.claude.json` and the login shell's `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` (spec §1 success 5);
    - a reference to a credential variable (`$ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `${ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN}`, `$AWS_BEARER_TOKEN_BEDROCK`, `$AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY`, `$AWS_SESSION_TOKEN`, `$CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN`);
    - `ps` with an environment flag (`-e`, `-E`, or `e` in a BSD-style word);
    - `compgen -e` and `compgen -v`.
14. **Route shapes.**
    - `POST /accounts` answers 201 with `{ account, signIn }`, where `signIn` is null for keyed kinds.
    - `PATCH` is `/accounts/:id`.
    - `GET /accounts/:id/usage` takes `?month=YYYY-MM` and defaults to the current month.
    - `GET /accounts/:id/identity` probes live and answers what Claude Code reports, plus the refusal a session would meet now.
    - `POST /accounts/:id/archive` is refused for the default account and for an account any project uses.
    - `PATCH /projects/:id` with a new `accountId` moves the project whatever work it has in flight, never to an archived account (decision 25). It takes `fallbackAccountId` and `usageHandoffAt` too (decision 29).
15. **Account labels are unique**, and the builder renames any account, the default one included, with `PATCH /accounts/:id`.
16. **Cards.** An account card folds per project on `account:<id>:<reason>`, with the problem appended for a refusal (`account:<id>:refused:<problem>`) and for a readiness card (`account:<id>:<problem>`); the default account's recorded-identity card folds on `account:<id>:recorded`. The cards about moving fold on `account:<id>:usage_high` (asked at a usage threshold), `account:<id>:moved` (an automatic move) and `account:<id>:available` (the move back), `<id>` being the account the project leaves or left. Its `detail.accountCard` holds `{ accountId, reason, problem, seenIdentity }`, plus `moves` (each option that moves the project, and the account it moves to), `moveBack` and `usage` (the report a threshold card is about) where they apply. A refusal card offers Resume ("I fixed it") and Later; one that names a seen subscription identity also offers Confirm. The recorded-identity card and the moved card offer nothing and hold no work. Answers go through the inbox's `onFlagAnswer`.
17. **Protocol.** Removing `billingMode`, `BillingMode` and the `provider.paused`/`provider.resumed` events is a breaking contract change, so `PROTOCOL_VERSION` becomes 3 (Task 12).
18. **`AccountDto`** carries `isDefault` and `secretSet`, and no sign-in command. The command names `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, which only `environment/` may name. It comes from `GET /accounts/:id/signin` and from `POST /accounts`.
19. **Aliases.** An account's `aliases` filter the profiles the router and escalation consider. A persona's pin and the unmeasured fallback are not filtered: the model check refuses a model the account can't serve.
20. **Identity probe.** `probeAccount` starts Claude Code with no prompt, in the data directory, with no setting sources and no MCP servers. It reads `accountInfo()` within 15 s, then ends the process.
21. **Credential errors mid-session.**
    - `authentication_failed`, `oauth_org_not_allowed` and `cloud_credential_error` are `signed_out`.
    - `billing_error` and `account_on_hold` are `cannot_bill`.
    - Each ends the session as an account refusal (decision 3).
22. **`insertSession` records the account the session launches on** and derives whether its cost is metered. The runner passes the account it built the launch for, because a project can move while a session waits for its start slot; any other caller passes none and gets the project's current account.
23. **Claude Code version warning.** The system check warns when the installed `claude` has a higher major version than 2.1.302, the version the account names were verified on (spec §10 point 4).
24. **The default account is labelled "Claude plan".** A label says what the account is, not who pays for it: a company may pay for the builder's plan, and `payer` records that. The builder can rename it. Plan prose and code call it the default account (`isDefault`), never by its label. Every picker and every card option that names an account shows its recorded identity beside the label, `Claude plan · you@example.com` (`accountDisplayName`); card titles use the label alone ("Claude plan runs as <email>").
25. **A project moves with work in flight.** A move to an archived account is refused. An unready or paused target is allowed: the work waits `QUEUED` there, and the preparer runs, as for any account.
    - A running session finishes on the account it started on. The runner reads the project's account before each session of a lane, so the move applies at each lane's next session.
    - The scheduler's next tick re-queues the project's `PAUSED_LIMIT` work, because only the new account's pause can hold it now.
    - Lane caps count a project's lanes against its current account, those still finishing on the old one included.
26. **Moving keeps the work, not the conversation.** Claude Code keeps a conversation in its account's config directory. So before a session, a lane whose next action would resume a conversation that ran on another account starts fresh instead (`nextActionOnAnotherAccount`, built on the crash path's `nextActionAfterCrash`):
    - `resume` becomes what a failed resume becomes: continue from the step's latest handoff, else start with "inspect git status first". When the lane's latest gate evidence failed, it becomes `fix` with that evidence instead, so the gate failure it was fixing reaches the fresh session.
    - `answer` keeps its inbox item and drops its provider session. A fresh answer session is briefed from the step's latest handoff before the builder's answer.
    - Committed work and the batch branch carry over. Context that was neither committed nor handed off is lost. The gate runs again before the step completes, so nothing unfixed passes.
27. **The usage-limit card offers the move.** A usage-limit card goes to each project that meets the pause. It offers "Continue on <account>" for each account the project could move to now (not archived, not its own, not paused), plus Later. The choice moves the project exactly as `PATCH` does.
    - A move withdraws the moved project's open cards that offered a move off the account it left (`usage_limit`, `usage_high`), and its open move-back card.
28. **The way back.** The project records the account it left in `projects.moved_from_account_id`, and when that account's limit resets in `move_back_at`, when it leaves an account because of a usage limit. That covers a usage-limit or threshold card, the automatic move to a fallback, and a `PATCH` while the old account is paused for its usage limit.
    - `move_back_at` is the pause's end, else the reset time the session reported, else the default pause length from now.
    - At `move_back_at` (re-armed at startup, and postponed while the account it left is still paused for its usage limit), one card per project asks "<label> is available again: move <project> back?". It offers Move back and Stay. Nothing moves until the builder answers, and any later move clears the record.
    - Two columns on the project, not an app setting. The record belongs to one project, changes in the same write as `account_id`, and must survive a restart. A threshold move leaves the old account unpaused, so the pause can't say when the account is free again; the reset time has to be kept with the move.
29. **The fallback account and the usage threshold.**
    - `projects.fallback_account_id` is optional. It is checked when set: another account than the project's own, not archived, and with a monthly cap when metered (decision 30). An account a project names as its fallback can't be archived. A move onto the fallback keeps it, and it applies again once the project moves back.
    - `projects.usage_handoff_at` is the threshold: `USAGE_HANDOFF_AT = 0.95` by default, 0.5 to 0.99.
    - The rate-limit event's `utilization` and `rateLimitType` reach the runner. A utilization outside 0 to 1 is a shape 10db hasn't seen, so it is dropped and no threshold fires.
    - Each crossing of a project's threshold on an account acts once per project and limit type; it acts again only after usage dropped below.
    - With a usable fallback (set, not its own, not archived, not paused, and capped when metered): each of the project's lanes running on the account gets the handoff nudge (once: a lane already handing off isn't nudged again), the project moves to the fallback, and a card that holds no work says what happened and why. Without one: a card asks, offering each account the project could move to, plus Later, and an answer nudges and moves the same way.
    - A hard limit (`rejected`) moves a project that has a usable fallback at once, with no nudge. The account still pauses as decisions 7 and 8 say, and its usage-limit card goes only to projects still on it.
    - Crossings are remembered in memory, so a restart while usage is past a threshold can ask once more.
30. **A metered fallback needs a monthly cap.** 10db moves work onto a fallback without asking, and only the cap bounds that spend. Setting a metered fallback that has no cap is refused (400). If the cap is removed later, the fallback is no longer used automatically: the builder is asked instead.

## New decisions

Task 13 appends these rows to `docs/architecture/decisions.md`, numbered consecutively from the next free number at merge, in the order shown. The code follows them from Task 1.

| Label | Decision                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | Why                                                                                                                                                            |
| ----- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| AC1   | A project binds one account (`projects.account_id`), which replaces its billing mode. An account is a database row: kind (`claude_subscription`, `anthropic_api_key`, `bedrock`, `gateway`; `openai_api_key` and `codex_signin` reserved and refused), a unique label, payer, expected identity, config directory, Keychain secret reference and fingerprint, settings (Bedrock region and profile, gateway base URL, model map, offered aliases), lane cap (default 4), monthly dollar cap (metered kinds) and `archived`. The daemon seeds the default account, labelled "Claude plan", on the builder's current login (the login shell's `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` when it sets one, else Claude Code's default directory) as the default for new projects; every other account, keyed kinds included, gets its own empty directory under `<dataDir>/accounts/<id>/claude`, mode 700.                                        | Accounts spec §3, §4. The default account can't be a new, signed-out directory, and a keyed session on its directory would read its saved login and config. |
| AC2   | `environment/`'s launcher is the only code that names account variables. A session's env is the login env with every billing switch stripped (now including `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` and `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN`, and for Bedrock the login shell's AWS credential, profile and region variables) plus its account's own. The gate, the shipping gate and repo MCP expansion get the project env, which has no account variables. Amends 44.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | Spec §3 "one module". A project's test commands and MCP servers never need the payer's credentials.                                                            |
| AC3   | Secrets live in the login Keychain as service `10db`, account `account:<acc_id>`. They are written through `security -i` on standard input and read back, then only a fingerprint (`sha256:` plus 16 hex digits) is stored. Each session start reads the secret and compares its fingerprint. The API returns `secretSet`, never a secret.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | Spec §3; spike 12: a gateway reports no organization, so a keyed account's identity is its key.                                                                |
| AC4   | A session checks itself before the prompt. `accountInfo()` must show the account's identity (the email for a subscription; the organization for an API-key or Bedrock account when one is set) and the credential its kind needs: subscription `firstParty` with a plan; API key `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`; Bedrock `bedrock`; gateway `tokenSource` `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN` on `firstParty`. The first `init` re-checks the key source and the model: an alias matches an ID containing `claude-<alias>`, a mapped ID matches exactly. Mid-session `authentication_failed`, `oauth_org_not_allowed`, `cloud_credential_error`, `billing_error` and `account_on_hold` are refusals too. Amends 47.                                                                                                                                                                                             | Spec §3, §5. Failing loudly on an unseen value is how a renamed variable shows up.                                                                             |
| AC5   | An account refusal pauses the account (reason `refused`) until the account is saved or its card is answered Confirm or Resume, and the workstream waits in `PAUSED_LIMIT` with its next action kept; on a subscription, an `accountInfo()` that never answers is a refusal as signed out. A model mismatch, a keyed account's missing `accountInfo()` or a repo settings problem still fails the workstream. Unready accounts (a subscription without a confirmed identity, a keyed account without a secret) never launch and are never paused: their work waits `QUEUED`, a lane that finds its account unready goes back to `QUEUED`, and the preparer probes at most once a minute, not while a Confirm card is open, and raises one card per project. The default account's identity is recorded from its first probe, with a card naming it; any other account's from the builder's Confirm. | Every lane on an account meets the same refusal, so failing each workstream would lose work and flood the inbox.                                               |
| AC6   | Concurrency and pauses are per account: `laneCap` replaces the per-provider cap, and a usage-limit pause, persisted as `account_pause.<id>`, stops only that account's lanes. A metered account with `monthlyCapUsd` is checked before every launch and before every further session in a lane; at or over the cap it pauses until the next local calendar month or a save, and its card's body shows the overshoot as running sessions end. A pause with no end outranks one that ends.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | Spec §3, §6. Stopping a running session would lose work; a session boundary is the natural place to stop.                                                      |
| AC7   | Usage is recorded per session (`account_id`, input, output and cached tokens, and `cost_usd`, null for subscriptions) and counted in the calendar month the session started, local time. `GET /accounts/:id/usage?month=YYYY-MM` sums sessions, in total and per project.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | Spec §3 "one source of truth".                                                                                                                                 |
| AC8   | On a subscription account, sessions and identity probes start one at a time, until the session reports `started` or `ended`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | Spec §10 fallback while the refresh race (Claude Code issues 54443, 61923) is unanswered.                                                                      |
| AC9   | In every step kind the guard refuses `security`, `osascript` naming the Keychain, environment dumps (`env` with nothing to run, `printenv` in any form, bare `set`, `export` or `export -p`, `declare` or `typeset` with `-x` or `-p` or nothing, `compgen -e` or `-v`, `ps` with an environment flag), any reference to a credential variable, and any tool or shell path under the data directory or the default account's sign-in (`~/.claude`, `~/.claude.json`, the login shell's `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | Spec §3 practical isolation: it stops agent mistakes and casual misuse, not deliberate attacks.                                                                |
| AC10  | `billingMode`, `BillingMode` and the `provider.paused` and `provider.resumed` events are removed and `PROTOCOL_VERSION` is 3.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | Contracts are additive except through a protocol bump (`packages/contracts/AGENTS.md`).                                                                        |
| AC11 | A project moves to another account with work in flight (`PATCH /projects/:id`, never to an archived account). A running session finishes on the account it started on, and `insertSession` records the account each session launched on. The runner reads the project's account before each session of a lane. A lane whose next action would resume a conversation from another account starts fresh, as after a failed resume (`nextActionOnAnotherAccount` over `nextActionAfterCrash`): from the step's latest handoff, else with "inspect git status first". A `resume` whose lane's latest gate evidence failed becomes `fix`. An `answer` keeps its inbox item and drops its provider session, and its fresh session is briefed from the handoff. Committed work and the batch branch carry over; context neither committed nor handed off is lost. | The builder runs out of a subscription mid-task and finishes on another account. Claude Code keeps a conversation in its account's config directory, so it can't be resumed elsewhere; the handoff is the continuity 10db already has. |
| AC12 | A usage-limit card offers "Continue on <account>" for each account the project could move to now (not archived, not its own, not paused), plus Later. A move off an account because of its usage limit records `projects.moved_from_account_id` and `move_back_at`. That covers a card, the automatic fallback, and a `PATCH` while the account is paused for its usage limit. At `move_back_at`, postponed while that account is still paused for its usage limit, one card per project asks to move it back (Move back, Stay). A move withdraws the project's open cards that offered one. | Moving away for a limit is temporary. The record lives on the project because it changes in the same write as `account_id`; a threshold move leaves the old account unpaused, so its reset time is kept with the move. |
| AC13 | A project may name `fallbackAccountId` (another account, not archived, capped when metered; an account named as a fallback can't be archived) and `usageHandoffAt` (default 0.95). The rate-limit event's `utilization` and `rateLimitType` reach the runner; a utilization outside 0 to 1 is dropped. When a session reports utilization at or above a project's threshold, once per crossing per project and limit type, a project with a usable fallback has its lanes on the account nudged to hand off, moves there, and gets a card that holds no work; one without is asked where to move, and an answer nudges and moves. A hard limit moves a project with a usable fallback at once. The account's pauses work as AC6 says. | The builder's workflow: max out the subscription, finish on Bedrock, move back. A handoff at 95% keeps the context a hard stop at 100% would lose. |
| AC14 | The seeded default account is labelled "Claude plan". Pickers and card options show an account as its label and recorded identity (`Claude plan · you@example.com`); card titles use the label. | A label says what the account is; who pays is `payer`, and a company may pay for the builder's plan. |

## Spec amendments

The code forced these changes to the accounts spec. Travis accepted all six, and this plan's PR applies them to the spec (amendment 2 to §4 and §5's launch table, 1 to §3 and §5, 3 to §4, 4 to §3 and §6, 5 to §7, 6 to §5). The same PR writes in Travis's later decisions: the default account's "Claude plan" label, and moving a project between accounts with work in flight, with a fallback account and the move back (§1, §3, §4, §5, §6, §7, §8 and §11).

1. **§3 and §5, model check timing.** Claude Code emits `init` only after the first prompt is sent, so "the model reported in `init` must equal the requested one" can't run before the prompt. The identity and key-source checks do run before the prompt. A model mismatch stops the session at its first turn, before any tool runs, but that turn's first request may already be billed. A model mismatch fails the workstream, not the account: the account's credentials were right, so pausing it would hold its other lanes for nothing. Wording for §3: "Any identity or credential mismatch refuses the session before the prompt, and nothing is billed; a model mismatch stops it at its first turn, before any tool runs, and fails the workstream, not the account."
2. **§4, `configDir`.** "Subscription only … created by 10db under the data directory" holds for neither the seeded default account nor a keyed one.
   - The default account is the builder's current login, which lives in the login shell's `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` when it sets one, and otherwise in Claude Code's default directory.
   - A keyed account on the default account's directory would read its `~/.claude.json`: its saved OAuth login, its key approvals and its config. Claude Code then arbitrates between that login and the account's key. The account check would catch a fallback (`wrong_credentials`), but the account could be unusable while the default account is signed in. An empty directory of its own removes the question.
   - Wording: "`configDir`: Claude Code's config directory for the account's sessions. For the seeded default account, the login shell's `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` when it sets one, else null (Claude Code's default directory, `~/.claude`). For every other account, keyed kinds included, an empty directory 10db creates under the data directory as `accounts/<acc_id>/claude`, with mode 700."
3. **§4, keyed identity.** Spike 12 found a gateway reports no organization, so keyed accounts need a stored key fingerprint. Add `secretFingerprint` (`sha256:` plus 16 hex digits, never the secret) to `accounts`. State that a gateway's `expectedIdentity` is always null.
4. **§6, "one card".** Inbox cards belong to a project and fold per project, so a cap or pause affecting an account bound to two projects raises one card in each project that meets it. Wording: "with one card per project that meets it".
5. **§7, route shapes.** `PATCH /accounts` becomes `PATCH /accounts/:id`. `POST /accounts` answers `{ account, signIn }`, because the sign-in command names `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, which only the launch module may name (§3).
6. **§5 "Signing in", for the default account only.** The plan records the default account's identity from its first probe without a confirmation step. §3 seeds the default account "for the builder's current login", and that login is what the probe reads. Every other subscription account still needs the builder's confirmation.
   - Its safety rests on amendment 2: the probe reads the directory the builder's own shell uses, and no keyed account shares it.
   - To show the builder which login it recorded, the preparer raises a card that holds no work: "Claude plan runs as <email>", with the email in its detail and the sign-in command for switching logins. A login switched afterwards meets an identity refusal whose card offers Confirm.

## Unverified, checked at runtime

Spike 12 verified the gateway names on Claude Code 2.1.302 and left these open. Each is guarded by a check that runs on every session and refuses, with a card that says what was seen, rather than bill the wrong payer.

| Not verified                                                                                                                                                                                       | The runtime guard                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | Task     |
| -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------- |
| Two config directories signed into different subscriptions stay separate in the Keychain, and `accountInfo()` reports each email (spike check 1).                                                  | Every session compares `accountInfo().email` with the account's confirmed email before the prompt. A shared Keychain entry shows the other email, and the session is refused, the account paused and a card raised. Task 14 runs the live check.                                                                       | 5        |
| Concurrent sessions on one config directory survive an OAuth token refresh (spike check 2: partial; open question).                                                                                | Subscription starts are staggered (decision 9). `authentication_failed` mid-session pauses the account with the sign-in fix instead of failing the work.                                                                                                                                                               | 5, 6     |
| Bedrock: `CLAUDE_CODE_USE_BEDROCK`, `AWS_REGION`, `AWS_PROFILE` or `AWS_BEARER_TOKEN_BEDROCK`, `apiProvider` `bedrock`, and the model IDs `init` reports.                                          | `apiProvider` must be `bedrock` before the prompt; the model check at `init` refuses a model outside the requested family.                                                                                                                                                                                             | 5        |
| API key: `apiKeySource` `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` on the account's own empty config directory while the default account is signed in, and whether `accountInfo()` reports an organization (spec §11 open question). | The key source is checked before the prompt and again at `init`; the key's fingerprint is checked at every start; the organization is compared only when the builder set one. A keyed session runs on its own directory, so the default account's saved login is not there to win over the key. Task 14 Step 2 runs it, required. | 3, 5, 14 |
| A gateway on a newer Claude Code might report `apiProvider` `gateway` (the SDK's type lists it).                                                                                                   | The check accepts only the values spike 12 saw, so a new value refuses with what it saw; the system check warns on a newer major version.                                                                                                                                                                              | 3, 5     |
| A signed-out config directory: whether `accountInfo()` answers with no email and no plan, rejects, or never answers.                                                                               | On a subscription all three read as signed out. Before a prompt, a missing `accountInfo()` refuses the session as `signed_out`: the account pauses and its card carries the sign-in command, and the lane does not fail. A probe that gets nothing raises the sign-in card. Task 14 Step 1 runs once signed out.       | 5, 6, 14 |
| Claude Code starts on an empty config directory with only an env key (API key, Bedrock, gateway) and asks nothing first: no onboarding, no key approval.                                           | The preflight's `accountInfo()` and the first `init` show what it bills; anything else refuses with what was seen. Task 14 Step 2 runs it.                                                                                                                                                                             | 3, 5, 14 |
| `security -i` stores a secret given on standard input exactly as given.                                                                                                                            | The write is read back and compared before the fingerprint is recorded; a mismatch answers 409 and records nothing.                                                                                                                                                                                                    | 3        |
| An identity probe with no prompt gets `accountInfo()` for keyed kinds (the preflight already does this before the prompt for subscriptions).                                                       | A probe that gets nothing within 15 s answers 409 (`GET /accounts/:id/identity`) or, for the preparer, raises the sign-in card with what it saw; nothing launches.                                                                                                                                                     | 5, 6     |
| Claude Code never hands an agent a path under its own config directory (for example a background task's output file), the default account's `~/.claude` included.                                             | The guard denies it and names the path, so a live run shows it; Task 14 watches for it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | 7, 14    |
| How often Claude Code sends `rate_limit_event` (the SDK says "when rate limit info changes"), and whether a session on an account that is close to its limit sends one before the limit hits. | The threshold acts on whatever arrives; a session that never reports one moves nothing early. The hard limit still pauses the account and moves a project with a usable fallback at once (Task 11's `rejected` path), and the usage-limit card offers the move. Task 14 Step 3 records what the Max plan sends. | 11, 14 |
| Whether `utilization` is present on a Max subscription's events, and its scale (the plan reads it as a fraction, 0 to 1). | A missing `utilization` crosses no threshold. One outside 0 to 1 is dropped as a shape 10db hasn't seen, so no threshold fires on a misread percentage. Either way the hard-limit path still moves the project. Task 14 Step 3 records the values seen. | 11, 14 |
| At what level `allowed_warning` first appears, and whether `rateLimitType` is always set. | The threshold reads `utilization`, never the status; only `rejected` acts on the status alone. A missing or unknown `rateLimitType` reads as "usage limit" on the card and crosses on its own key. Task 14 Step 3 records them. | 11, 14 |
| Whether a threshold event carries `resetsAt`. | Without it, `move_back_at` is the default pause length from the move, and the move-back card is postponed while the old account is still paused for its usage limit. | 10, 11 |

## Global Constraints

Everything in M0's, M1a's, M1b's, M2a's and the batch delivery plan's Global Constraints still applies. On top of that:

- **Branch:** `feat/accounts`, cut from `main` once the batch delivery plan and M2b Part A have both merged. Open one PR to `main` at the end; its title is a Conventional Commit. Until Task 5 sessions are checked against the billing mode as before. From Task 5 to Task 6, an account with no confirmed identity runs without the email comparison, and Task 6 makes such an account unready. From Task 2 to Task 9, a project moved with work in flight tries to resume its conversations on the new account's config directory, where Claude Code can't find them, and recovers through the crash path; Task 9 starts those lanes fresh. The branch ships as one PR.
- **Commits:**
  - Each commit is one command that names its paths: `git add <path> … && git commit -m '…'`. Never `git add -A`, `git add .` or `git commit -a`.
  - The scope is one of `daemon`, `desktop`, `contracts`, `db`, `harness`, `repo`, `ci`, `docs`, `deps`. The subject is lowercase and the header is at most 100 characters.
  - Every message ends with the attribution footer your own session's instructions give (for example a `Co-Authored-By:` line and a session link), after a blank line, one extra `-m` paragraph per line. The commit commands below write it as `-m '<attribution>'`: replace that with one `-m` per footer line (for example `-m 'Co-Authored-By: …' -m 'Claude-Session: …'`), never both lines in one `-m`. Never copy another session's link.
  - Run `pnpm format` first. A failing hook leaves the paths staged: fix the cause and rerun the same line. Never `--no-verify` or `LEFTHOOK=0`.
- **Generated files** are regenerated, never edited: `pnpm --filter @10db/contracts generate` after a contracts change; the migration baseline is regenerated after a schema change (Task 2 Step 5, Task 10 Step 3, Task 11 Step 3 and Task 12 Step 4). Never merge `packages/db/drizzle/` or `packages/contracts/generated/` textually.
- **Contracts are additive until Task 12.** Tasks 1–11 only add schemas, fields and event types. Task 12 removes `billingMode`, `BillingMode` and the provider events and bumps `PROTOCOL_VERSION` to 3.
- **IDs** are minted only through `mintId`. New kind: `Account` (`acc`).
- **Every state change writes its row and its event in one transaction** (`state.write`). `account.saved` on every account insert or change (its data names the changed fields, never a value), `account.paused` and `account.resumed` on pauses, and `account.cap_reached` in the same transaction as a cap pause. The desktop's `INVALIDATIONS` maps `account` (Task 12).
- **Secrets never leave the Keychain except into a session's env.** Not into the database (a fingerprint only), logs, events, cards, API responses, error messages or test output. Logs never carry an email or organization either (decision 47's rule): refusal messages name the account's label and what kind of value differed, and the seen identity travels only in a card's title, body and `detail`.
- **Only `environment/` names account variables** (`CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`, `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`, `CLAUDE_CODE_USE_BEDROCK`, `AWS_REGION`, `AWS_PROFILE`, `AWS_BEARER_TOKEN_BEDROCK`). Other modules use `SECRET_VARIABLES`, `signInCommand` and `Launcher.loginConfigDir()` from it. Two exceptions set no variable:
  - The repo-settings refusal families in `sessions/project-settings.ts` are prefixes, not account variables, and stay where they are.
  - `sessions/account-check.ts` and the fake adapter compare the `apiKeySource` and `tokenSource` values Claude Code reports, which happen to spell variable names.
- **No `as` casts, no `!`, no `any`** except at a validated boundary with a comment (`.claude/rules/code-style.md`). Exhaustive switches over `AccountKind`/`SupportedAccountKind`, `AccountProblem` and `AccountPauseReason`.
- **Time:** months are computed in the machine's local time from the injected `Clock` (`accounts/month.ts`); nothing calls `Date.now()` or `new Date()` without an argument in logic.
- **Tests** (`.claude/rules/testing.md`):
  - Red first, with the expected failure written in each step.
  - Real SQLite in a temp directory. Usage and cap tests give the store the test's clock (`createTestStore(() => clock.now())`), so session start times and the month agree.
  - The Keychain is faked at the `Keychain` interface (`createMemoryKeychain()` in `apps/daemon/test/fixtures.ts`); `environment/keychain.test.ts` covers the real `security` boundary with an injected `exec`.
  - The fake adapter is the Claude Code boundary; it runs the same `checkAccount` as the real adapter.
  - Table-driven for each kind's launch, the account checks, the model match, the month windows, pause merging, the scheduler rows and the guard verdicts.
  - Month tests build instants with the local `Date` constructor (`new Date(2026, 9, 31, 23, 30)`), so they pass in every time zone.
- **Tunable values are named constants:** `DEFAULT_ACCOUNT_LANE_CAP = 4`, `PREPARE_RETRY_MS = 60_000`, `IDENTITY_PROBE_MS = 15_000`, `ACCOUNT_NAMES_VERIFIED_ON = '2.1.302'`, `FINGERPRINT_HEX = 16`, `USAGE_HANDOFF_AT = 0.95`.
- **knip:** an `index.ts` re-exports only what another module or a test imports.
- **Comments never carry decision numbers**; they cite the spec (`accounts spec §6`) or say the why in words.

## Review Focus

The conditions most likely to bite a person, each with the task whose tests pin it:

1. **A session runs on the wrong payer.**
   - Case: two subscriptions share a Keychain entry, so config directory B reports A's email; the builder re-ran `/login` in the default account's directory as another account; a client's key was swapped in the Keychain by hand; the login shell exports `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` or `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN`; a keyed session would find the default account's saved login in `~/.claude.json`.
   - No prompt is sent; the account is paused with a card naming what was seen; the work waits in `PAUSED_LIMIT` and resumes after the builder confirms or fixes the account.
   - Tests: Task 3 (`strips every billing switch and 10db variable, and adds no account credential`; the launch rows `an API key on its own directory` and `a gateway with a model map`, which set the account's own `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`; the launch refusal row `a secret changed outside 10db`), Task 8 (`gives a keyed account its own empty config directory, and no sign-in command`), Task 5 (the `checkAccount` rows `another subscription in the Keychain` and `an API key where the subscription should be`; `pauses the account and sends no prompt when the identity differs, then resumes after Resume`; `refuses an API-key account whose Keychain secret changed, before any session row`), Task 6 (`asks the builder to confirm an account on its own directory, and Confirm records it`).
2. **A metered account crosses its monthly cap.**
   - Case: three lanes run when the cap is reached; the month rolls over at local midnight; the builder raises the cap; a lane hands off after the cap.
   - No new session starts, running ones finish, the card shows the overshoot as they end, the next month or a raised cap resumes the work, and a lane stops at its next session boundary.
   - Tests: Task 4 (`pauses at the cap until the next local month, with one card and account.cap_reached`, `rewrites the cap card with the overshoot as running sessions end`, `resumes when the cap is raised`, `launches nothing on a metered account at its monthly cap, and launches under it`, `stops a lane at its next session once its account reaches the monthly cap`).
3. **A secret leaks.**
   - Case: an API response, an event, a log line or a card carries the key; an agent runs `env`, `printenv`, `echo $ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `security find-generic-password`, `ps eww`, or reads `~/Library/Application Support/10db/accounts/…`, `~/.claude.json` or `~/.claude/.credentials.json`, including through `sh -c` and `$(…)`, in a read-only step too; the gate prints `$ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`.
   - The secret appears nowhere outside the Keychain and the session's env; the guard denies each command with a reason; the gate never sees the key.
   - Tests: Task 8 (`takes a secret into the Keychain and never returns, emits or logs it`), Task 7 (`account isolation`: `denies %s in an implement step and a read-only step`, `denies %s on a protected path in both step kinds`), Task 5 (`gives the gate the project env, never the account's key`).
4. **A path with a space reaches a shell.**
   - Case: the default data directory is `~/Library/Application Support/10db`, so a config directory path has a space and can hold a quote.
   - The sign-in command quotes it as one shell word; the guard still recognises the path written with an escaped space or quotes.
   - Tests: Task 3 (`signInCommand`: `quotes %s as one shell word`), Task 7 (rows `the data directory, with escaped spaces`, `the data directory, partly quoted`, `the data directory through a repo link`), Task 8 (`creates a subscription with a private config directory and a sign-in command quoting its path`).
5. **Credentials expire mid-run.**
   - Case: a subscription's OAuth token can't refresh, or a Bedrock SSO session expires, while sessions run.
   - The session ends as an account refusal, the account pauses with the exact fix (`CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR='…' claude /login` or `aws sso login --profile <p>`), and the work resumes the same conversation after Resume.
   - Tests: Task 5 (`ends a session that reports authentication_failed as signed out, before any tool runs`, `resumes the same conversation after credentials expired mid-session`), Task 6 (`starts one session at a time on a subscription account`).
6. **Two projects share one account.**
   - Case: projects A and B both bind a lane-cap-2 account with work queued in each; one project's session hits the usage limit.
   - At most two lanes run across both projects; the pause holds both projects' lanes on that account and no other account's.
   - Tests: Task 4 (`caps lanes per account across two projects`, `holds only the paused account's projects`, `re-queues only the paused account's work`).
7. **A fresh install starts work before the default account's identity is known.**
   - Case: the builder creates a project and starts a task on a new data directory, signed in or not.
   - The work waits `QUEUED` while the preparer probes the default account's directory (the login shell's `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, else the default), records the email it finds, raises a "Claude plan runs as <email>" card that holds nothing, and launches. A signed-out directory, including one whose `accountInfo()` answers nothing, raises one card with `claude /login`, and 10db probes again at most once a minute.
   - Tests: Task 5 (`start.test.ts`'s `seeds the default account on the login shell's own Claude Code directory`; the `a preflight that gets no account` table), Task 6 (`records the default account's identity from its first probe of %s, with a card that holds nothing, then asks for a tick`, `raises one sign-in card when the default directory is signed out, and probes again only after a minute`, `asks the default account to sign in when its probe gets no account in time`, and `start.test.ts`'s `records the default account's identity from its first probe, then runs the task`).
8. **An account turns unready while its work runs.**
   - Case: the builder clears a subscription's identity with `PATCH` while a lane hands off to its next session; a Confirm card for the account is already open.
   - The lane goes back to `QUEUED` with its next action kept and asks the preparer; the account is not paused and no refusal card appears. The preparer doesn't probe again while the Confirm card is open.
   - Tests: Task 6 (`re-queues a lane whose subscription account lost its identity, and asks the preparer`, `probes no more while a Confirm card waits on the builder`).
9. **Moving accounts mid-work.**
   - Case: the subscription hits its 5-hour limit while two lanes run, one of them fixing a gate failure and one parked on a question. The builder moves the project to Bedrock from the usage-limit card, answers the question there, and moves back when the card asks. A project with a fallback crosses 95% mid-step. A fallback is a metered account with no cap.
   - No lane resumes a conversation from another account's config directory. Each lane's next session runs on the new account, briefed from its latest handoff, and the gate failure and the answer reach it. A running session finishes where it started, and each session's row names the account it ran on. At 95% the lanes hand off before the move; at the limit itself the project moves at once. Nothing moves back until the builder says so. An uncapped metered fallback is refused, and never taken without asking.
   - Tests: Task 9 (`nextActionOnAnotherAccount` rows; `starts a fresh session from the handoff when the project moved off the account its conversation ran on`, `brings the builder's answer to a fresh session after the project moved`, `keeps the gate failure a lane was fixing when the project moved`, `runs a lane's next session on the account its project moved to while the first one ran`; `briefs a fresh session from the handoff before the builder's answer`), Task 10 (`offers to continue on each account the project could move to, and moves it when one is chosen`, `asks once the limit resets whether to move back, and moves back on Move back`, `waits while the account it left is still paused, and asks when that pause ends`, `remembers nothing for a plain move, and moves nothing when another field of the change is refused`), Task 11 (`hands off and moves a project with a fallback once its threshold is crossed`, `asks once per crossing when the project has no usable fallback, and moves it when the builder answers`, `takes no fallback that is paused or metered without a cap, and asks instead`, the fallback refusal rows, `account-moves.test.ts`'s `hands a lane off and finishes the task on the fallback when the subscription nears its limit` and `moves to the fallback at the hard limit and finishes the task there from the handoff`).

---

## File Structure

```
packages/contracts/src/
  accounts.ts          AccountKind, SUPPORTED_ACCOUNT_KINDS, isMetered, needsSecret, AccountProblem,
                       AccountPauseReason, MODEL_ALIAS_PATTERN, settings schemas, parseAccountSettings, AccountInput, AccountPatch,
                       AccountDto, AccountCreatedDto, AccountSecretInput, AccountSignInDto, AccountRefusal,
                       AccountIdentityDto, Month, UsageTotals, AccountUsageDto, ACCOUNT_CARD_DETAIL,
                       AccountCardDetail (+ moves, moveBack, usage), AccountCardChoice (+ MoveBack, Stay),
                       accountDisplayName, USAGE_HANDOFF_AT (+ accounts.test.ts)
  ids.ts               (+ Account: acc)
  events.ts            (+ AccountSaved, AccountCapReached, AccountPaused, AccountResumed; Task 12 drops the provider events)
  entities.ts          (+ ProjectInput/ProjectDto/ProjectPatch.accountId, fallbackAccountId, usageHandoffAt;
                       ProjectDto.movedFromAccountId, moveBackAt; Task 12 drops billingMode)
  system.ts            (+ SystemCheckDto.claudeCode)
  states.ts            (Task 12 drops BillingMode)
  protocol.ts          (Task 12: 3)
packages/db/src/
  schema.ts            (+ accounts; projects.account_id, moved_from_account_id, move_back_at, fallback_account_id,
```

---

### Task 1: Contracts for accounts, their cards and their usage

New vocabularies and schemas, standalone and additive. No existing schema changes shape here: `ProjectDto` gains `accountId` in Task 2 with the table it reads from, and `billingMode` goes in Task 12.

**Files:**

- Create: `packages/contracts/src/accounts.ts`, `packages/contracts/src/accounts.test.ts`
- Modify: `packages/contracts/src/ids.ts`, `packages/contracts/src/events.ts`, `packages/contracts/src/index.ts`, `packages/contracts/src/json-schema.ts`
- Generated: `packages/contracts/generated/*.schema.json`

**Interfaces:**

- Consumes: nothing new.
- Produces (all exported from `@10db/contracts`):
  - `AccountId` (`acc_<uuidv7>`), `mintId('Account')`.
  - `AccountKind` (`claude_subscription`, `anthropic_api_key`, `bedrock`, `gateway`, `openai_api_key`, `codex_signin`), `SUPPORTED_ACCOUNT_KINDS`, `type SupportedAccountKind`, `isMetered(kind: AccountKind): boolean`.
  - `AccountProblem` (`unconfirmed`, `identity_mismatch`, `wrong_credentials`, `signed_out`, `secret_missing`, `secret_changed`, `cannot_bill`), `AccountPauseReason` (`usage_limit`, `monthly_cap`, `refused`).
  - `accountDisplayName(account: { label: string; expectedIdentity: string | null }): string`: the label, and the recorded identity beside it (`Claude plan · you@example.com`).
  - `DEFAULT_ACCOUNT_LANE_CAP = 4`, `DEFAULT_ACCOUNT_LABEL = 'Claude plan'`, `MODEL_ALIAS_PATTERN = /^[a-z]+$/` (the one alias pattern: the model map's keys, `aliases`, and Task 5's model check).
  - `SubscriptionSettings`, `ApiKeySettings`, `BedrockSettings`, `GatewaySettings`, `AccountSettings` (union), `parseAccountSettings(kind: SupportedAccountKind, raw: unknown): AccountSettings`, `needsSecret(kind: SupportedAccountKind, settings: AccountSettings): boolean`.
  - `AccountInput` (discriminated on `kind`; `type AccountInput = z.input`, `type AccountConfig = z.output`), `AccountPatch`, `AccountDto`, `AccountSignInDto`, `AccountCreatedDto`, `AccountSecretInput`, `AccountRefusal`, `AccountIdentityDto`, `Month`, `UsageTotals`, `AccountUsageDto`.
  - `ACCOUNT_CARD_DETAIL = 'accountCard'`, `AccountCardDetail`, `AccountCardChoice` (`Confirm`, `Resume`, `Later`).
  - `EventType.AccountSaved` (`account.saved`), `AccountCapReached` (`account.cap_reached`), `AccountPaused` (`account.paused`), `AccountResumed` (`account.resumed`).

```ts
describe('account kinds', () => {
  it.each<[AccountKind, boolean]>([
    [AccountKind.ClaudeSubscription, false],

    { name: 'an API key', kind: 'anthropic_api_key', settings: {}, secret: true },

      kind: 'bedrock',
      settings: { region: 'us-west-2', awsProfile: 'p' },

});

describe('the other account schemas', () => {

    expect(AccountPatch.safeParse({}).success).toBe(false);

      'Claude plan · you@example.com',

    expect(accountDisplayName({ label: 'OpenRouter', expectedIdentity: null })).toBe('OpenRouter');
```
