```ts
import {
  AccountCreatedDto,
  AccountDto,
  InboxItemDto,
  ProjectDto,
  TaskDto,
  WorkstreamDto,
} from '@10db/contracts';
import { listSessions } from '@10db/db';
import { afterEach, beforeEach, describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
import { z } from 'zod';
import { startDaemon, type RunningDaemon } from '../src/daemon';
import { FakeAdapter, type FakeScriptBook } from '../src/sessions';
import { apiClient, quickFlowId } from './api-client';
import { createMemoryKeychain, createTempRepo, handoffArgs, tempDir, waitForAsync } from './fixtures';

const QUIET = { caffeinate: () => null, watch: false } as const;

let data: { path: string; cleanup(): void };
let repo: { path: string; cleanup(): void };
let daemon: RunningDaemon | null;
beforeEach(() => {
  data = tempDir('account moves');
  repo = createTempRepo();
  daemon = null;
});
afterEach(async () => {
  await daemon?.stop();
  repo.cleanup();
  data.cleanup();
});

/**
 * A fresh daemon with a confirmed second subscription, and a project on the default account that
 * names it as its fallback; runs one task to Done and reports where its sessions ran.
 */
async function runOnFallback(book: FakeScriptBook) {
  const adapter = new FakeAdapter(book);
  const running = await startDaemon({
    dataDir: data.path,
    port: 0,
    adapter,
    logLevel: 'warn',
    keychain: createMemoryKeychain(),
    ...QUIET,
  });
  daemon = running;
  const api = apiClient(running.url, running.token);
  const { account: fallback } = await api.post('/accounts', AccountCreatedDto, {
    kind: 'claude_subscription',
    label: 'Second plan',
  });
  await api.patch(`/accounts/${fallback.id}`, AccountDto, {
    expectedIdentity: 'second@example.com',
  });
  const project = await api.post('/projects', ProjectDto, {
    name: 'demo',
    repoPath: repo.path,
    gateCommand: 'true',
    flowId: await quickFlowId(api),
    areas: [{ name: 'src', globs: ['src/**'] }],
    fallbackAccountId: fallback.id,
  });
  const task = await api.post(`/projects/${project.id}/tasks`, TaskDto, {
    title: 'Alpha',
    acceptanceCriteria: ['it works'],
    areas: ['src'],
  });
  await api.post(`/tasks/${task.id}/start`, z.unknown());
  const done = await waitForAsync(async () =>
    (await api.get('/workstreams?active=false', z.array(WorkstreamDto))).find(
      (w) => w.taskId === task.id && w.state === 'DONE',
    ),
  );
  return {
    adapter,
    fallback,
    home: project.accountId,
    project: (await api.get('/projects', z.array(ProjectDto))).find((p) => p.id === project.id),
    titles: (await api.get('/inbox', z.array(InboxItemDto))).map((i) => i.title),
    ranOn: listSessions(running.state.db, done.id).map((s) => s.accountId),
  };
}

describe('moving to a fallback account', () => {
```

In `packages/db/src/store/projects.ts` (import `isMetered` from `@10db/contracts`):

```ts
/**
 * A fallback is another account than the project's own, not archived, and capped when metered:
 * 10db moves work onto it without asking, and only the cap bounds that spend.
 */
function checkFallback(ctx: WriteContext, accountId: AccountId, fallbackId: AccountId | null): void {
  if (fallbackId === null) return;
  if (fallbackId === accountId)
    throw new InvalidError("A project's fallback is another account than its own");
  const fallback = getAccountRow(ctx.tx, fallbackId);
  if (fallback.archived) throw new InvalidError(`Account "${fallback.label}" is archived`);
  if (isMetered(fallback.kind) && fallback.monthlyCapUsd === null)
    throw new InvalidError(
      `Set a monthly cap on "${fallback.label}" before making it a fallback: 10db moves work onto it without asking`,
    );
}
```

`insertProject` calls `checkFallback(ctx, accountId, rest.fallbackAccountId)` once it has the account. `patchProject` calls `checkFallback(ctx, changes.accountId ?? current.accountId, changes.fallbackAccountId)` only when `changes.fallbackAccountId !== undefined`: a move onto the fallback keeps it, and it applies again once the project moves back.

```ts
const info = msg.rate_limit_info;
const overage = info.isUsingOverage === true;
// A share of the limit; a value outside 0 to 1 is a shape 10db hasn't seen, so no threshold fires on it.
const utilization =
  info.utilization !== undefined && info.utilization >= 0 && info.utilization <= 1
    ? info.utilization
    : null;

    resetsAt: resetsAtToDate(overage ? (info.overageResetsAt ?? info.resetsAt) : info.resetsAt),
    utilization,
    limitType: info.rateLimitType ?? null,
  },
];
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Write the limits, the fallback and their cards**

In `apps/daemon/src/accounts/cards.ts` (import `type UsageReport` from `./limits`):

```ts
export const USAGE_HIGH_CARD = 'usage_high';
export const MOVED_CARD = 'moved';

const LIMIT_NAMES: Readonly<Record<string, string>> = {
  five_hour: '5-hour limit',
  seven_day: 'weekly limit',
  seven_day_opus: 'weekly Opus limit',
  seven_day_sonnet: 'weekly Sonnet limit',
  seven_day_overage_included: 'weekly limit',
  overage: 'extra-usage limit',
};

/** How a card names the limit a session reported; one 10db hasn't seen reads as the usage limit. */
export function limitName(limitType: string | null): string {
  return (limitType === null ? undefined : LIMIT_NAMES[limitType]) ?? 'usage limit';
}

/** "is at 96% of its 5-hour limit", or "reached its 5-hour limit" at the limit itself. */
function standing(report: UsageReport): string {
  const limit = limitName(report.limitType);
  if (report.status === 'rejected' || report.utilization === null) return `reached its ${limit}`;
  return `is at ${String(Math.round(report.utilization * 100))}% of its ${limit}`;
}

function usageDetail(report: UsageReport): Record<string, Json> {
  return {
    usage: {
      limitType: report.limitType,
      utilization: report.utilization,
      resetsAt: report.resetsAt?.toISOString() ?? null,
    },
  };
}

/** The project had no usable fallback when it crossed its threshold: the builder picks where it goes. */
export function usageHighCard(
  project: ProjectDto,
  account: AccountRow,
  targets: readonly AccountDto[],
  report: UsageReport,
): AccountCard {
  const moves = targets.map((t) => ({ choice: `Move to ${accountDisplayName(t)}`, accountId: t.id }));
  const only = targets.length === 1 ? targets[0] : undefined;
  const where = only ? only.label : 'another account';
  const resets = report.resetsAt ? ` It resets at ${report.resetsAt.toISOString()}.` : '';
  return {
    title:
      moves.length > 0
        ? `${account.label} ${standing(report)}: hand off and move ${project.name} to ${where}?`
        : `${account.label} ${standing(report)}`,
    body: `A session on ${account.label} reported that it ${standing(report)}.${resets} ${
      moves.length > 0
        ? `Moving asks each lane of ${project.name} on ${account.label} to hand off, then runs its next sessions on the account you pick, and 10db asks whether to move back once the limit resets.`
        : `No other account can take ${project.name} now, so it stays on ${account.label} until the limit.`
    }`,
    foldKey: accountFoldKey(account.id, USAGE_HIGH_CARD),
    detail: detail(account, null, null, null, { moves, ...usageDetail(report) }),
    ...(moves.length > 0
      ? {
          options: [
            ...moves.map((m) => ({
              label: m.choice,
              description: `Hand off and run ${project.name} there`,
            })),
            { label: AccountCardChoice.Later, description: `Stay on ${account.label} for now` },
          ],
        }
      : {}),
  };
}

/** 10db moved the project to its fallback on its own; the card says what and why, and holds no work. */
export function movedCard(
  project: ProjectDto,
  account: AccountRow,
  fallback: AccountDto,
  report: UsageReport,
): AccountCard {
  const handedOff = report.status === 'rejected' ? '' : ' after asking its running lanes to hand off';
  return {
    title: `${project.name} moved to ${fallback.label}: ${account.label} ${standing(report)}`,
    body: `A session on ${account.label} reported that it ${standing(report)}. ${project.name} names ${accountDisplayName(fallback)} as its fallback, so 10db moved it there${handedOff}. Each lane's next session runs on ${fallback.label} from its latest handoff; work neither committed nor handed off stays behind. 10db asks whether to move back once ${account.label}'s limit resets.`,
    foldKey: accountFoldKey(account.id, MOVED_CARD),
    detail: detail(account, null, null, null, usageDetail(report)),
  };
}
```

In `apps/daemon/src/accounts/moves.ts` (import `isMetered` from `@10db/contracts`, `getAccount`, `getAccountRow` from `@10db/db`, `USAGE_HIGH_CARD` from `./cards`):

- `MoveWhy`'s usage-limit variant gains `/** Whether running lanes on the old account are asked to hand off first. */ readonly handOff: boolean;`.
- `createAccountMoves`'s options gain `/** Asks the project's lanes running on the account to hand off (the runner's handOff). */ handOff(projectId: ProjectId, accountId: AccountId, reason: string): void;`.
- In `move`, before the write: `if (why.cause === MoveCause.UsageLimit && why.handOff) opts.handOff(projectId, from, `moving to ${getAccountRow(db, to).label}`);`.
- `withdrawMoot`'s keys gain `accountFoldKey(from, USAGE_HIGH_CARD)`.
- Add:

```ts
/**
 * The fallback 10db may move a project to without asking: set, not its own account, not archived,
 * not paused, and capped when metered, because only the cap bounds unattended spend.
 */
export function usableFallback(
  db: DbReader,
  project: ProjectDto,
  isPaused: (id: AccountId) => boolean,
): AccountDto | null {
  if (project.fallbackAccountId === null || project.fallbackAccountId === project.accountId)
    return null;
  const fallback = getAccount(db, project.fallbackAccountId);
  if (fallback.archived || isPaused(fallback.id)) return null;
  if (isMetered(fallback.kind) && fallback.monthlyCapUsd === null) return null;
  return fallback;
}
```

`apps/daemon/src/accounts/limits.ts`:

```ts
import type { AccountId, ProjectId } from '@10db/contracts';
import { getAccountRow, listProjects } from '@10db/db';
import type { StateStore } from '../events';
import type { InboxService } from '../inbox';
import { movedCard, usageHighCard, type AccountCard } from './cards';
import { MoveCause, moveTargets, usableFallback, type AccountMoves } from './moves';
import type { AccountPauses } from './pauses';

/** What a session reported about its account's usage limit (Claude Code's rate-limit event). */
export interface UsageReport {
  readonly status: 'allowed' | 'allowed_warning' | 'rejected';
  /** The share of the limit used, 0 to 1; null when Claude Code didn't report one. */
  readonly utilization: number | null;
  /** `five_hour`, `seven_day` and the rest; null when Claude Code didn't say. */
  readonly limitType: string | null;
  readonly resetsAt: Date | null;
}

export interface AccountLimits {
  /**
   * A session on the account reported its usage limit. Past a project's threshold, the project hands
   * its lanes off and moves to its fallback, or the builder is asked; at the limit itself a project
   * with a fallback moves at once. A crossing acts once per project and limit until usage drops back.
   */
  usageReported(accountId: AccountId, report: UsageReport): void;
}

export function createAccountLimits(opts: {
  readonly state: StateStore;
  readonly inbox: InboxService;
  readonly pauses: Pick<AccountPauses, 'pausedFor'>;
  readonly moves: Pick<AccountMoves, 'move'>;
}): AccountLimits {
  const db = opts.state.db;
  /** Project, account and limit past their threshold: in memory, so a restart may ask once more. */
  const crossed = new Set<string>();
  const isPaused = (id: AccountId): boolean => opts.pauses.pausedFor(id) !== null;
  const raise = (projectId: ProjectId, card: AccountCard): void => {
    opts.inbox.flag({
      projectId,
      workstreamId: null,
      title: card.title,
      body: card.body,
      detail: card.detail,
      foldKey: card.foldKey,
      ...(card.options ? { options: card.options } : {}),
    });
  };

  return {
    usageReported(accountId, report) {
      const account = getAccountRow(db, accountId);
      const atLimit = report.status === 'rejected';
      for (const project of listProjects(db)) {
        if (project.accountId !== accountId) continue;
        if (!atLimit) {
          if (report.utilization === null) continue;
          const key = [project.id, accountId, report.limitType ?? ''].join(':');
          if (report.utilization < project.usageHandoffAt) {
            crossed.delete(key);
            continue;
          }
          if (crossed.has(key)) continue;
          crossed.add(key);
        }
        const fallback = usableFallback(db, project, isPaused);
        if (fallback) {
          // At the threshold the lanes hand off first; at the limit itself nothing is left to do it with.
          opts.moves.move(project.id, fallback.id, {
            cause: MoveCause.UsageLimit,
            resetsAt: report.resetsAt,
            handOff: !atLimit,
          });
          raise(project.id, movedCard(project, account, fallback, report));
        } else if (!atLimit) {
          raise(project.id, usageHighCard(project, account, moveTargets(db, project, isPaused), report));
        }
      }
    },
  };
}
```

In `apps/daemon/src/accounts/card-answers.ts` (import `AccountPauseReason`), the move's `why` for a card that isn't a move back:

```ts
{
  cause: MoveCause.UsageLimit,
  resetsAt: card.data.usage?.resetsAt ? new Date(card.data.usage.resetsAt) : null,
  // The usage-limit card's lanes were stopped by the pause; a threshold card's still run.
  handOff: card.data.reason !== AccountPauseReason.UsageLimit,
}
```

In `apps/daemon/src/accounts/pauses.ts`, `at.card` becomes `AccountCard | null`, and `pause` raises the card only when there is one (`if (at.card) opts.inbox.flag({ … })`). In `runtime.ts`, `rateLimited` passes `card: null` for a project that has left the account: `project.accountId === accountId ? usageLimitCard(…) : null`, with the comment "A project that moved to its fallback has nothing waiting on this account."

`apps/daemon/src/accounts/index.ts` adds `createAccountLimits`, `type AccountLimits`, `type UsageReport`, `usableFallback` (trim to what knip finds imported).

- [ ] **Step 6: Hand lanes off from the runner**

In `apps/daemon/src/runner/runner.ts`:

- `RunnerOptions.accounts` adds `& Pick<AccountLimits, 'usageReported'>` (type import from `../accounts`).
- `Control` gains `readonly projectId: ProjectId;` (set in `launch` from the workstream's row) and `/** Set while a session runs: asks it to hand off once. */ requestHandoff: ((reason: string) => void) | null;` (null in `launch`).
- In `runSession`, the context branch's nudge becomes a closure the account side can call too:

```ts
/** Asks the session to hand off, once: its context filled, or its account neared its usage limit. */
const requestHandoff = (reason: string): void => {
  if (st.handoffRequested || st.outcome) return;
  st.handoffRequested = true;
  opts.state.write((ctx) => {
    if (getWorkstreamRow(ctx.tx, id).state === S.Running)
      transitionWorkstream(ctx, id, { to: S.HandingOff, fence: ctl.fence, reason });
  });
  handle?.send(readOnly ? HANDOFF_NUDGE_READ_ONLY : HANDOFF_NUDGE);
};
```

  The `context` case calls `requestHandoff(`context at ${String(event.percentage)}%`)` where it transitioned and sent the nudge itself, and keeps its firm-nudge branch. Set `ctl.requestHandoff = requestHandoff;` beside `ctl.handle = handle;`, and `ctl.requestHandoff = null;` beside `ctl.handle = null;`.
- The `rate_limit` case reports every event before it acts on a rejected one:

```ts
case 'rate_limit':
  // Logged without any identity: what a Max plan reports here is not yet verified.
  log.info(
    { status: event.status, utilization: event.utilization, limitType: event.limitType },
    'usage reported',
  );
  // First, so a project that moves to its fallback is off the account before the pause raises its card.
  opts.accounts.usageReported(ctl.accountId, {
    status: event.status,
    utilization: event.utilization,
    limitType: event.limitType,
    resetsAt: event.resetsAt,
  });
  if (event.status === 'rejected' && !st.outcome) {
    // …as before: the paused outcome, and opts.accounts.rateLimited(ctl.accountId, event.resetsAt, project.id)
  }
  break;
```

  and the `ended` handler's `RateLimited` arm calls `opts.accounts.usageReported(ctl.accountId, { status: 'rejected', utilization: null, limitType: null, resetsAt: null })` before its `rateLimited`.
- `Runner` gains:

```ts
  /** Asks the project's lanes whose session runs on the account to hand off; a lane already asked isn't asked again. */
  handOff(projectId: ProjectId, accountId: AccountId, reason: string): void;
```

  implemented as `for (const c of active.values()) if (c.projectId === projectId && c.accountId === accountId) c.requestHandoff?.(reason);`.

In `apps/daemon/src/daemon/start.ts`, `createAccountMoves` gets `handOff: (projectId, accountId, reason) => { runner?.handOff(projectId, accountId, reason); }`. After the moves:

```ts
const limits = createAccountLimits({ state, inbox, pauses: accountPauses, moves });
```

and the runner's `accounts` becomes `{ ...accounts, prepare: preparer.prepare, usageReported: limits.usageReported }`.

In `apps/daemon/test/engine.ts`, the engine's moves get the same `handOff` through its late-bound runner, it builds `createAccountLimits` over them, and the runner's `accounts` gains `usageReported: (accountId, report) => { usageReports.push({ accountId, report }); limits.usageReported(accountId, report); }`, with `usageReports` exposed as `engine.usageReports`. `rateLimited` still only records, so a usage limit pauses nothing in the engine, as before.

### Task 12: Retire billing modes, and pick a project's account in the UI

Nothing reads `billingMode` any more (Task 5). This task removes it from the contracts and the schema, along with the provider pause events. That is a breaking contract change, so `PROTOCOL_VERSION` becomes 3. The desktop gets the two account pickers the removal forces: the new-project form's Account select replaces Billing, and Settings' Billing section becomes the project's account picker (accounts spec §7, "Project settings get an account picker"). Billing also gets the fallback picker (Task 11). Every picker names an account by its label and recorded identity (`accountDisplayName`, decision 24). The Accounts screen itself stays a planned slot for the next plan.

The inbox needs no change for the account cards: it already renders any card's options and posts the chosen label, and the daemon tests in Tasks 10 and 11 pin the options' text and what each answer does.

```ts
it("shows the selected project's areas, hazards and account, moves it, and names its fallback", async () => {
  const project = projectDto({
    areas: [{ name: 'api', globs: ['src/api/**'] }],
    hazards: [{ name: 'deps', globs: ['**/package.json'], commands: ['pnpm add'] }],
  });
  const client = accountDto({
    kind: 'anthropic_api_key',
    label: 'Client key',
    configDir: '/data/accounts/k/claude',
  });
  const patched: unknown[] = [];
  server.use(
    http.get(daemonUrl('/projects'), () => HttpResponse.json([project])),
    http.get(daemonUrl('/accounts'), () => HttpResponse.json([DEFAULT_ACCOUNT, client])),
    http.patch(daemonUrl('/projects/:projectId'), async ({ request }) => {
      const body = await request.json();
      patched.push(body);
      // The daemon refuses an uncapped metered fallback; the move itself goes through.
      return typeof body === 'object' && body !== null && 'fallbackAccountId' in body
        ? HttpResponse.json(
            {
              error: 'invalid',
              message:
                'Set a monthly cap on "Client key" before making it a fallback: 10db moves work onto it without asking',

  fireEvent.click(await screen.findByRole('option', { name: 'Client key (Anthropic API key)' }));

    expect(patched).toEqual([{ accountId: client.id }]);

  const fallback = screen.getByRole('textbox', { name: 'Fallback account' });
  expect(fallback).toHaveProperty('value', '');
  fireEvent.click(fallback);
  fireEvent.click(await screen.findByRole('option', { name: 'Client key (Anthropic API key)' }));
  await waitFor(() => {
    expect(patched).toEqual([{ accountId: client.id }, { fallbackAccountId: client.id }]);
  });
  expect(await screen.findByText(/Set a monthly cap on "Client key"/)).toBeTruthy();
});
```

```ts
import {
  accountDisplayName,
  AccountKind,
  type AccountDto,
  type SupportedAccountKind,
} from '@10db/contracts';

/** How each account kind reads in the UI. */
export const ACCOUNT_KIND_LABEL: Readonly<Record<SupportedAccountKind, string>> = {
  [AccountKind.ClaudeSubscription]: 'Claude subscription',
  [AccountKind.AnthropicApiKey]: 'Anthropic API key',
  [AccountKind.Bedrock]: 'Amazon Bedrock',
  [AccountKind.Gateway]: 'Gateway',
};

/**
 * The accounts a project can run on: every one not archived, by label, recorded identity and kind,
 * as in "Claude plan · you@example.com (Claude subscription)".
 */
export function accountOptions(
  accounts: readonly AccountDto[],
): { value: string; label: string }[] {
  return accounts.filter((a) => !a.archived).map((a) => ({
    value: a.id,
    label: `${accountDisplayName(a)} (${ACCOUNT_KIND_LABEL[a.kind]})`,
  }));
}
```

```ts
  return useQuery({

    queryFn: async () => readJson(await client.accounts.$get(), AccountList),
  });
}
```

`apps/desktop/src/views/settings/project-billing.tsx` becomes:

```tsx
import { AccountId, type ProjectDto } from '@10db/contracts';
import { Alert, Select, Stack, Text } from '@mantine/core';
import { useAccounts, usePatchProject } from '../../daemon/queries';
import { accountOptions } from './account-kind-label';

/**
 * Which account pays for the project, and the fallback 10db moves it to near a usage limit
 * (accounts spec §6, §7). Work in flight follows a move at each lane's next session.
 */
export function ProjectBilling({ project }: { readonly project: ProjectDto }) {
  const accounts = useAccounts().data ?? [];
  const patch = usePatchProject(project.id);
  return (
    <Stack gap="xs">
      <Select
        label="Account"
        description="Who pays for this project's sessions. Work in flight moves with it: each lane's next session runs on the new account."
        data={accountOptions(accounts)}
        value={project.accountId}
        allowDeselect={false}
        disabled={patch.isPending}
        onChange={(value) => {
          if (value !== null && value !== project.accountId)
            patch.mutate({ accountId: AccountId.parse(value) });
        }}
      />
      <Select
        label="Fallback account"
        description="Where 10db moves the project, after a handoff, when its account nears its usage limit. A metered fallback needs a monthly cap."
        placeholder="None"
        data={accountOptions(accounts).filter((o) => o.value !== project.accountId)}
        value={project.fallbackAccountId}
        clearable
        disabled={patch.isPending}
        onChange={(value) => {
          if (value !== project.fallbackAccountId)
            patch.mutate({ fallbackAccountId: value === null ? null : AccountId.parse(value) });
        }}
      />
      {patch.error ? (
        <Alert color="red" title="Couldn't change the project's accounts">
          {patch.error.message}
        </Alert>
      ) : null}
      <Text size="sm" c="dimmed">{`Default model: ${project.model}@${project.effort}`}</Text>
    </Stack>
  );
}
```

In `apps/desktop/src/views/settings/sections.ts`, the Accounts slot's `planned` text becomes "Each billing account with its kind, verified identity and this month's usage against its cap, and the sign-in steps for subscriptions. A later accounts plan fills it; until then accounts are created through the daemon's API (see the README)."

### Task 13: Architecture section, decisions, open items, README and the gate

**Files:**

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the architecture section**

`docs/architecture/03-accounts.md` (`tests/repo/src/architecture-docs.test.ts` checks the three header lines):

```markdown
# 03 Accounts

- **Status:** Locked
- **Scope:** Who pays for each project's sessions: accounts, how a session proves it runs on its account, per-account concurrency, pauses and monthly caps, usage, moving a project between accounts with work in flight, and how agents are kept from credentials.
- **Summary:** The design is `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-17-10db-accounts-design.md`, with spike 12's findings in `docs/architecture/research/spike-12-accounts.md`; its decisions and the plan's are rows <first>–<last> of `decisions.md`. Changes are new rows that name what they supersede.

## Where it lives

| Module                                      | Job                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| ------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `packages/db` `accounts`, `store/usage.ts`  | one row per account; projects and sessions bind to one; usage is a query over sessions                                                                                                                   |
| `apps/daemon/src/environment/`              | the Keychain (`keychain.ts`) and the launcher (`launch.ts`): the only code that names account variables; the project env for gates and repo MCP servers; the Claude Code version warning                 |
| `apps/daemon/src/sessions/account-check.ts` | what a session must report before its prompt and at its first `init`; both adapters use it                                                                                                               |
| `apps/daemon/src/accounts/`                 | pauses, the monthly cap, readiness and the preparer, staggered subscription starts, cards and their answers, the service behind the HTTP routes, moving a project (`moves.ts`), the move back (`move-back.ts`) and the fallback at a usage threshold (`limits.ts`) |
| `apps/daemon/src/runner/`                   | reads the project's account before each session; a lane whose conversation ran on another account starts fresh from its handoff (`next-action.ts`); `handOff` nudges a project's lanes on an account |
| `apps/daemon/src/guard/account-rules.ts`    | Keychain commands, environment dumps, credential variables, the data directory and the default account's sign-in (`~/.claude`, `~/.claude.json`, the login shell's config directory) are refused in every step kind |
| `apps/desktop`                              | the new-project form's account, and Settings' Billing account and fallback pickers; the Accounts screen is the next plan                                                                                  |

## Invariants

- A session sends its prompt only after `accountInfo()` matched the account's identity and credential kind; a mismatch pauses the account with a card and sends nothing. A model mismatch fails the workstream, not the account.
- The default account runs on the builder's own login (the login shell's `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, else Claude Code's default directory); every other account, keyed kinds included, runs on its own config directory under the data directory.
- An unready account (no confirmed identity, no secret) is prepared, never refused: its work waits `QUEUED`.
- A keyed account's secret is read from the Keychain at every start and must match the fingerprint 10db recorded; it is never in the database, a log, an event, a card or a response.
- An account's lanes, usage limits and monthly cap apply only to that account; a capped or refused account lets running sessions finish.
- The gate and the repo's MCP servers never get an account's credentials.
- Nothing billing-related comes from the login shell.
- On a subscription account, sessions start one at a time until the refresh question in `open-questions.md` is answered.
- A project is on exactly one account at a time. A move applies at each lane's next session; no lane resumes a conversation from another account's config directory, it starts fresh from its latest handoff.
- Nothing moves a project without the builder except its own fallback, which must be capped when metered; the way back is always the builder's choice.
```

Replace `<first>` and `<last>` with the numbers Step 2 assigns.

| Whether one person may hold several personal Claude subscriptions (for example plus-addressed emails), one per client | Anthropic's consumer terms, or support                         | relying on per-client personal subscriptions; Team seats or client API keys are the alternative  |
| Whether `accountInfo()` reports an organization for API-key and Bedrock sessions                                      | spike 12 `keyed api_key` and `keyed bedrock` (plan 09 Task 14) | comparing an expected organization; until then a keyed account's identity is its key fingerprint |
| Whether two config directories signed into different subscriptions stay separate in the Keychain                      | spike 12 `identity` with two subscriptions (plan 09 Task 14)   | trusting per-client subscriptions on one macOS user; the email check refuses a mix-up meanwhile  |

- [ ] **Step 5: Document accounts in the README**

In `README.md`, before "## Model data from Artificial Analysis", add:

````markdown
## Accounts

Every project runs on an account: who pays for its sessions. A fresh 10db has one, **Claude plan** (the default account), which is
the Claude Code login you already use (`claude /login`, in `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` if your shell sets it).
10db records its email the first time a task runs, tells you in the inbox which email it recorded
("Claude plan runs as …"), and refuses any session that reports a different one.

Until the Accounts screen ships, add other accounts through the daemon's API, with the token in
`~/Library/Application Support/10db/ui-token` and the port in `daemon.json` beside it:

```sh
T=$(cat ~/Library/Application\ Support/10db/ui-token)
P=$(jq .port ~/Library/Application\ Support/10db/daemon.json)
# A second Claude subscription: 10db prints the sign-in command for its own config directory.
curl -s -H "Authorization: Bearer $T" -H 'content-type: application/json' \
  -d '{"kind":"claude_subscription","label":"Company A"}' http://127.0.0.1:$P/accounts
# A client's API key, capped at $500 a month; the key goes to your login Keychain, never to 10db's database.
curl -s -H "Authorization: Bearer $T" -H 'content-type: application/json' \
  -d '{"kind":"anthropic_api_key","label":"Client B","monthlyCapUsd":500}' http://127.0.0.1:$P/accounts
curl -s -X PUT -H "Authorization: Bearer $T" -H 'content-type: application/json' \
  -d '{"secret":"sk-ant-…"}' http://127.0.0.1:$P/accounts/<id>/secret
```

After you sign a new subscription in, 10db asks you in the inbox to confirm the email it found. Pick a
project's account in Settings, Billing. Sessions on an account run under its own lane cap and usage limits; a
metered account stops starting sessions at its monthly cap until the next month or a raised cap.
`GET /accounts/<id>/usage?month=YYYY-MM` shows its tokens and dollars per project.

A project can move to another account in Settings, Billing, even with work in flight. A running session
finishes where it is, and each lane's next session runs on the new account, fresh from its latest handoff;
work that was neither committed nor handed off stays behind. When an account hits its usage limit, its inbox
card offers to continue on another account, and once the limit resets 10db asks whether to move back. Name a
fallback account in Billing and 10db moves the project there by itself when its account reaches 95% of a usage
limit (`usageHandoffAt` changes that), after asking its running lanes to hand off. A metered fallback needs a
monthly cap, because nobody approves that spend.
````

- [ ] **Step 6: Update the daemon's AGENTS.md**

In `apps/daemon/AGENTS.md`:

- Add `accounts` to the module order, after `sessions, inbox, briefing, team` and before `mcp`.
- Replace the `environment/` bullet's last sentence and the "Sessions and the gate both run in `sessionEnv(project)`" bullet with:

```
- `environment/` owns the login env (memory only), the Keychain, the launcher and the system check. Nothing else reads the login env, and only `launch.ts` names account variables (`CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, …); other modules use `SECRET_VARIABLES`, `signInCommand` and `Launcher.loginConfigDir()`. Never write the login env or a secret to disk, the database or logs.
- The guard's protected paths (`guard/account-rules.ts`) are the data directory and the default account's sign-in; `startDaemon` builds them once and passes them to the runner and the MCP handler.
- A session runs in its launch env (the project env plus its account's variables); the gate, the shipping gate and the repo's MCP servers run in the project env (the login env with every billing switch stripped). Nothing from the daemon's own `process.env` reaches a project's commands.
- `accounts/` owns account pauses (`account_pause.<id>`), the monthly cap, readiness and the preparer, staggered subscription starts and the account service. The scheduler and the runner ask `AccountRuntime.blocked` before every launch and every further session in a lane. Months are local time on the injected clock.
- `accounts/` also moves projects between accounts. `PATCH /projects/:id` and the cards go through `AccountMoves.move` (`moves.ts`), which remembers a move off a usage-limited account; `move-back.ts` asks to move back when that limit resets; `limits.ts` moves a project to its fallback at its usage threshold. The runner reads the project's account before each session and never resumes a conversation that ran on another account: `runner/next-action.ts` starts the lane fresh from its handoff.
- Both adapters check a session's account with `sessions/account-check.ts` before the prompt and at the first `init`; a refusal ends the session as `account_refused`, which pauses the account. Logs never carry an email or organization.
```

- [ ] **Step 7: Run the gate**

Run: `make gate`
Expected: the last line is `GATE PASS format lint typecheck test build e2e drift knip rust (…)`.

- A `knip` failure names an export nothing imports: remove it from its `index.ts`, or use it.
- A `drift` failure names a generated file: regenerate it and add it to the commit that owns its source.
- If only `e2e` fails because ports 4318 or 1420 are busy, wait for them to free (`lsof -i :4318 -i :1420` prints nothing), then rerun.


- the `GATE PASS` line;
- the decisions recorded;

- that a project moves between accounts with work in flight, by hand, from its usage-limit card or to its fallback, and that a lane continues from its handoff, not its conversation;

It ends with the attribution lines the session asks for.

### Task 14: [Travis] Live checks on real accounts

Run by Travis, after Task 13's PR is green and before it merges. The automated suites cover the checks through the fake adapter and a scripted SDK. This run is spec §8's manual live check. It also finishes the spike 12 checks that need real accounts, and records what Claude Code's rate-limit events report, which the usage threshold depends on. Each check uses a few cents of usage, except Step 4, which runs a task on a metered account.

**Files:**

- Modify (at the end): `docs/architecture/research/spike-12-accounts.md`, `docs/architecture/open-questions.md`, `docs/open-items.md`

- [ ] **Step 1: Two subscriptions in parallel, each verified**

In the 10db checkout on the PR branch, run `rm -rf .dev/data && pnpm dev`. Then:

1. Create a subscription account through the API (README, "Accounts"). Bind a second project to it and start a task there **before** signing it in. Expected: the work waits `QUEUED` and nothing fails; one "Sign in the account …" card carries the `signIn.command`. Note in the spike document whether the probe saw no email, no answer within 15 s, or a rejection.
2. Run the `signIn.command` in a terminal and sign in as a second Claude subscription. Within a minute the inbox shows "Confirm the account …" with the second email. Answer Confirm.
3. Start a task in a project on the default account. Its identity is recorded, and the inbox shows "Claude plan runs as <your email>"; the task doesn't wait for it.
4. With both confirmed, start one more task on each.

Expected:

- The tasks on the two accounts run at the same time and reach Done.
- `daemon.log`'s `claude session billing` lines show `apiProvider: firstParty` and no email.
- `GET /accounts/<id>/usage` lists each project under its own account.
- Run `/login` in the second account's directory as the default account's email. Its next session is refused with "Claude Code reported a different email", the card names the email, and its work waits in `PAUSED_LIMIT`.
- Then sign that directory out (`CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR='<dir>' claude /logout`) and answer Resume. The next session is refused as signed out, before any prompt, with the sign-in command on the card; the account pauses and the workstream waits in `PAUSED_LIMIT`. It does not fail.

- [ ] **Step 2: An API key (required), and Bedrock if you have it**

Keep the default account signed in throughout: a keyed session beside a saved OAuth login is the case this checks.

1. Create an `anthropic_api_key` account with `monthlyCapUsd` set to `0.05` and `PUT` its key. Bind a project to it and start a task.
2. If you have Bedrock access, do the same with a `bedrock` account on an SSO profile.

Expected:

- `ls -A "<dataDir>/accounts/<id>/claude"` was empty before the first session. The session runs on the key, with no onboarding or key-approval prompt and no `wrong_credentials` refusal. `GET /accounts/<id>/identity` shows `apiKeySource: ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, and the organization if `accountInfo()` reports one. Record both in the spike 12 document.
- After the first session the account pauses at its cap with one card. Raising the cap with `PATCH` resumes it.
- Bedrock: run `aws sso logout`. The next session is refused with `aws sso login --profile <p>`.

- [ ] **Step 3: What the Max plan's rate-limit events report**

Through Steps 1 and 4, and over a day of ordinary work on the default account (a Max subscription), the runner logs each rate-limit event as `usage reported` with its status, utilization and limit type, and nothing else. Read them with `grep -n 'usage reported' <dataDir>/logs/daemon.log`.

Expected, recorded in the spike 12 document:

- how often the event arrives: every turn, only when something changes, or not until a warning;
- whether `utilization` is present, and on what scale (the plan reads a share from 0 to 1);
- the utilization at which `allowed_warning` first appears, and the `limitType` values seen.

If `utilization` never arrives, or arrives on another scale, the guard drops it: no threshold card appears and nothing moves before the hard limit. Then add a row to the open items and tell Travis, because only the hard-limit move works until the threshold reads what Claude Code sends.

- [ ] **Step 4: Move a project mid-step to a metered account, and back**

Use the API-key account from Step 2, or the Bedrock one, with its cap raised to a few dollars by `PATCH`. On a project on the default account, start a task that takes several turns, for example two files committed one at a time with a test each.

1. While its lane is RUNNING, move the project to the keyed account in Settings, Billing.
2. Once a session runs on the keyed account, move the project back.
3. If the default account reaches a usage limit during the day, answer its card's "Continue on <account>" instead, let the task finish there, and answer the move-back card when it comes. Then set the keyed account as the project's fallback (`PATCH /projects/<id>` with `fallbackAccountId`) for the next limit, and record what happens at the threshold.

Expected:

- Each move: the running session finishes on the account it started on, and the lane's next session runs on the other one. `GET /accounts/<id>/usage` lists the project under both accounts.
- The session after each move starts fresh: `sqlite3 <dataDir>/10db.db "select sequence, account_id, provider_session_id from sessions where workstream_id = '<ws_…>' order by sequence"` shows a new provider session on each account, and the workstream never passes through `RECOVERING` (no failed resume). Its first activity continues from the handoff, or inspects the working tree when there was none.
- Commits made before the move are on the branch after it; the task reaches Done.
- A usage-limit card offers the keyed account; after the limit resets, one card asks "Claude plan is available again: move <project> back?", and nothing moves until it is answered.
- With the fallback set, the threshold or the hard limit moves the project with a card that says which limit, how full, and why; at the threshold the lanes hand off first.

- [ ] **Step 5: Watch for the guard and the version**

Through the runs, `grep -n -E "data directory|sign-in" ~/Library/Application\ Support/10db/logs/daemon.log` prints no denial of a path Claude Code itself handed an agent. If one shows up, add it to the open items. The UI shows no Claude Code version warning on 2.1.x.

- [ ] **Step 6: Record the results**

Update `docs/architecture/research/spike-12-accounts.md`:

- mark `identity` and `keyed api_key` (and `keyed bedrock` if run) VERIFIED or FAILED, with the evidence lines, with emails masked as the spike masks them;
- record how a signed-out directory answered `accountInfo()` (no email, no answer, or a rejection) and that the API key ran on its own empty directory while the default account was signed in;
- record Step 3's rate-limit findings and Step 4's moves, with the session rows;
- update the open-questions rows Task 13 added, the rate-limit row included;
- tick "Accounts: the manual live checks" in `docs/open-items.md`, with the date.

```bash
git add docs/architecture/research/spike-12-accounts.md docs/architecture/open-questions.md docs/open-items.md && git commit -m 'docs(docs): record the accounts live checks' -m '<attribution>'
```

If two subscriptions reported the same email in Step 1, the email check refused the mix-up as designed. Separating them is then spec §10's fallback: one macOS user per subscription account. That fallback needs its own plan, and the result goes to Travis before this PR merges.

## Self-review notes

- **Spec coverage:**
  - §1 success 1 (a mismatched identity never sends a prompt): Tasks 5 (the `checkAccount` table, adapter and runner cases) and 6 (readiness). Success 2 (accounts in parallel, their own limits and lane caps): Task 4. Success 3 (tokens and dollars this month per project): Tasks 2 and 8. Success 4 (the monthly cap): Task 4. Success 5 (credentials out of reach): Tasks 2 and 3 (every account but the default one on its own config directory), 5 (the project env) and 7 (the guard, the default account's sign-in included). Success 6 (a project moves with work in flight, and on its own to a fallback near a usage limit): Tasks 9, 10 and 11.
  - §3 decisions: every row maps to a task:
    - the account row: Task 2;
    - subscriptions on config directories (the default account on the login's own, every other account on 10db's): Tasks 2, 3, 5 and 8;
    - keyed secrets: Tasks 3 and 8;
    - the launch module: Task 3;
    - the model map: Task 3;
    - the self-check: Task 5;
    - per-account concurrency: Task 4;
    - the monthly cap: Task 4;
    - usage: Task 2;
    - aliases: Task 6;
    - practical isolation: Task 7;
    - accounts starting fresh: Tasks 2, 5 (the default account on the login shell's directory) and 6 (its identity, and the card naming it); its "Claude plan" label: Task 1, and the identity beside a label in pickers and card options: Tasks 1, 10, 11 and 12;
    - moving a project between accounts: Task 9 (lanes follow, fresh from the handoff), Task 10 (the usage-limit card's move, the move back) and Task 11 (the fallback and the usage threshold).
  - §4 data: Task 2, Tasks 10 and 11 for the project's move and fallback columns, and Task 12 for `billingMode`. §5 launching and checking, expired-credential cards and signing in: Tasks 3, 4 (fix texts), 5, 6 and 8. §6: Tasks 2 and 4, and moving accounts in Tasks 9, 10 and 11. §7 routes: Task 8, with the project's fallback fields in Task 11. §7 Settings: the project's account and fallback pickers are in Task 12; the Accounts screen is the next plan. §8 testing: launch-spec table (Task 3), mismatch refusals on the fake adapter (Task 5), guard tables (Task 7), scheduler lane cap, scoped pause and month rollover (Task 4), secrets in responses (Task 8), moves between accounts (Tasks 9, 10 and 11), manual live check (Task 14). §10 point 4: Task 3. §11's conversation-transplant spike is not planned here; Task 13 records it.
  - §9 (pre-release schema policy and IDs): already on `main` (decisions 67, 68); Tasks 2, 10, 11 and 12 follow it.
- **Gaps checked and closed while writing:**
  - The default account must exist before Task 6, or `POST /projects` fails from Task 2 on: Task 2 seeds it in `startDaemon`.
  - The refused card needs a way back after a fix that changes nothing in 10db, such as `/login` or `aws sso login`: the Resume choice (Tasks 1 and 4).
  - The gate saw the session env with its key before this plan: decision 10 and Task 5's gate case.
  - The default account was pointed at `~/.claude` even when the login shell moves the login with `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`: Task 2's seed takes a `configDir`, Task 3's `loginConfigDir()` reads it, and Task 5 seeds the default account on it.
  - Keyed accounts ran on the default account's config directory, where a saved OAuth login competes with the key: every created account gets its own (Tasks 2, 3 and 8; amendment 2), and Task 14 Step 2 runs an API key beside a signed-in default account.
  - The guard covered the data directory but not the default account's own sign-in: Task 7's protected paths add `~/.claude`, `~/.claude.json` and the login shell's directory. A mention must end where the path ends, so siblings such as `~/.claude-work` and `<data>-backup` stay allowed.
  - An unready account hit by a lane was refused and paused, so the preparer never ran: the runner's `unready` outcome re-queues and calls the preparer (Task 6).
  - A signed-out directory may answer nothing: a subscription's missing `accountInfo()` is `signed_out`, before a prompt (Task 5) and in a probe (Task 6).
  - Task 6 made `confirm` required on Task 4's `createAccountCardAnswers` calls: Task 6 updates `runtime.test.ts`, `start.ts` and `engine.ts`.
  - The pauses test never reached the restore of an expired pause, because the first instance's timer fired: the first instance is stopped before the clock moves (Task 4).
  - A move with work in flight would have resumed conversations in a config directory that doesn't hold them: Task 9 starts those lanes fresh, and `insertSession` records the account a session launched on, which a move during its start-slot wait could otherwise mislabel.
  - A refusal from a session that ran on the old account would have paused the new one: Task 5's `held` arm names `ctl.accountId`, the account the session ran on.
  - A gate failure the lane was fixing was lost when a paused fix session's `resume` started fresh: the lane's latest failed gate evidence turns it into `fix` (Task 9). An answer would have been dropped: it keeps its inbox item and is briefed with the handoff (Task 9).
  - Moving from a card withdrew the card being answered, so the inbox couldn't record the answer: the move skips the card it answers (`answering`, Task 10).
  - At the hard limit, a project that moved to its fallback would still have got the usage-limit card: the pause raises no card for a project no longer on the account (Task 11), and the project moves first.
  - Lanes stopped by the old account's pause after the move would have waited for that pause: the scheduler re-queues `PAUSED_LIMIT` work by the project's current account, and `onMoved` asks for a tick (Tasks 4 and 10).
  - A threshold move leaves the old account unpaused, so the pause's end can't trigger the move back: `move_back_at` keeps the reported reset (Task 10, decision 28).
  - Tasks 10 and 11 made `move`, `stay` and `handOff` required: they update the earlier tests that build the card answers and the moves.
  - Task 5's `start.ts` seed read `.value` off `StateStore.write`, which returns the value itself.
